## Researcher–agent collaboration



Chronological shared conversation. The researcher sees these handoffs, not private tool output. Full tool and model records follow.

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the staged repository and relevant files for the capital PCA analysis, especially any scripts/notebooks and the structure/contents of data/capital.csv. Report what you find about the PCA workflow (including how variables are binned into 100-year slices, inputs/preprocessing, and any available PC1/PC2 scores or loadings relevant to HouseSize, Dagger, and HatchetSword), and propose a concise analysis approach to answer the question. Flag consequential assumptions or uncertainties, such as PCA orientation/sign conventions or whether the question concerns variable loadings versus time-slice scores. Do not yet execute the full analysis or state a conclusion.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The brief identifies capital.csv variables and confirms 100-year bins, but provides no PCA results, implementation details, or expected findings. Initial inspection should clarify the available workflow and ambiguity before authorizing the main analysis.

**Open objectives:** Inspect repository and data assets relevant to capital PCA; Clarify PCA workflow and distinction between variable loadings and time-slice scores; Identify consequential assumptions and propose analysis approach

**Agent reply (plan)**

Repository inspection found only `task.json` and the three staged CSVs—no PCA script or notebook documenting the original workflow. `data/capital.csv` has 39 rows (BCE 4300 down to 500 at 100-year intervals) and 15 columns: BCE, group, color, and 12 archaeological Z-indicator fields. Metadata describes group/color as dropped after loading, while the numeric fields represent forms of capital. The CSV uses comma decimal separators in numeric fields, so pandas reads these fields as strings by default. No explicit nulls were reported in the parsed table, though numeric conversion should validate every value.

Proposed next step after approval: inspect/parse all 12 indicators using decimal-comma conversion, then compute PCA across indicators using rows as 100-year time slices. Report the three requested variables' signed PC1 loadings and their PC2 loadings/distribution, and optionally provide time-slice scores as separate evidence if the question means scores rather than variable loadings. Check explained variance and sensitivity to centering/scaling, since source columns are already labeled Z values but their precise standardization basis is undocumented. Keep PCA sign orientation explicit because component signs can flip without changing the PCA solution.

**Shared evidence:**

`find` showed only three data CSVs and task.json, with no scripts or notebooks. Reading capital.csv via pandas gave shape (39, 15), BCE min/max 500/4300 and 39 unique years; head rows show 100-year increments. Pandas inferred all capital indicators as strings because values such as `-0,44` use decimal commas. Task metadata maps ZAxtSchwert to hatchets/swords, ZDolch to daggers, Zhausgr to house size and says group/color are dropped.

**Assumptions:** Each row is a century-level time slice and the 12 Z-indicator columns are the active PCA variables.; Reconstructing PCA from the supplied capital.csv is an acceptable proxy in absence of original code.

**Uncertainties:** The original PCA standardization, missing-value policy, and component orientation are undocumented.; Question wording could refer to variable loadings (how variables align with components) or temporal score distribution; must report and label these separately.

### 2. Researcher — next_task

Proceed with the proposed analysis using the 39 century rows and 12 archaeological indicators. Parse decimal-comma values and validate completeness; center the input and report a documented primary PCA choice, noting whether you use the supplied Z-values directly or additionally scale them. Give signed PC1 and PC2 variable loadings (or clearly identified correlations/contributions, with the exact convention) for HouseSize, Dagger, and HatchetSword, plus explained variance. Also summarize their distribution on PC2 and provide time-slice scores separately if useful to disambiguate the question. State any sign convention used and retain the caveat that the original PCA preprocessing/orientation is undocumented; do not present reconstructed results as the source study's exact PCA.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** The inspection report indicates no original analysis code, a 39-row century-binned capital table, and decimal-comma indicator values. The proposed ambiguity between variable loadings and temporal scores is consequential; reporting both clearly labeled alongside preprocessing and sign convention directly addresses it.

**Open objectives:** Compute and report data-grounded PCA results for the three symbolic-capital indicators on PC1 and PC2.; Clarify PC2 distribution and distinguish variable loadings from temporal score distribution.; Document reconstruction preprocessing, explained variance, and PCA sign ambiguity.

**Agent reply (result)**

I reconstructed PCA from `capital.csv`; these are not asserted to be the source study's exact PCA because its preprocessing and orientation are undocumented. The file contains 39 century rows and 12 indicators. Decimal-comma conversion succeeded for every cell (0 missing or nonfinite values). The primary PCA centers the supplied Z-value columns but does not rescale them again: their sample SDs are already near 1 for most indicators, although `ZSichel` is 0.538. Using the centered covariance matrix, PC1 explains 31.84% of variance and PC2 explains 20.42% (52.26% combined).

Loadings below are signed eigenvector coefficients of the centered covariance PCA (unit-length eigenvectors, not variable-score correlations). I chose the otherwise arbitrary sign of each reported component so HouseSize's coefficient is positive on both PC1 and PC2:

| Indicator | PC1 coefficient | PC2 coefficient |
|---|---:|---:|
| HouseSize (`Zhausgr`) | +0.2283 | +0.3644 |
| Dagger (`ZDolch`) | +0.2793 | +0.1195 |
| HatchetSword (`ZAxtSchwert`) | +0.3010 | +0.0666 |

Thus the three indicators all have positive PC1 coefficients, with HatchetSword and Dagger somewhat larger than HouseSize. On PC2, HouseSize has the strongest positive alignment; Dagger is more modestly positive, and HatchetSword is close to zero but still positive under this orientation. PC2 scores across the 39 time slices span −3.788 to +2.414 (median +0.393; quartiles −0.807 and +0.891). The score distribution is broadly mixed, with the three earliest slices (4300–4100 BCE) at the negative extreme and positive scores common over much of the middle of the series; later slices include both signs. These are time-slice scores, distinct from variable loadings. For illustration, the aligned PC1/PC2 score pairs at 4300, 3500, 2100, and 500 BCE are (−1.706, −3.788), (−0.650, +2.040), (+0.238, −0.566), and (+0.815, −1.684), respectively.

A sensitivity PCA with additional unit-sample-SD scaling gives similar requested-variable loadings (PC1: +0.2251, +0.2656, +0.2986; PC2: +0.3274, +0.0742, +0.0540 for HouseSize, Dagger, HatchetSword respectively) and explains 30.33% and 19.47% on PC1/PC2. The PC2 pattern is therefore robust to this particular scaling choice, though original study settings remain unknown.

**Shared evidence:**

Executed a numpy eigendecomposition PCA script against the staged CSV. Numeric validation reported shape (39, 12), 0 missing, 0 nonfinite. The primary centered, unscaled covariance PCA yielded variance ratios 0.318408 and 0.204184; requested loadings and temporal PC scores are listed above. A unit-SD scaling sensitivity check yielded variance ratios 0.303278/0.194656 and preserved the positive PC1/PC2 relationships.

**Assumptions:** Each data row represents one 100-year time slice, as indicated by its BCE sequence.; The 12 indicators, rather than BCE/group/color, are PCA input variables.; The supplied Z-values are the preferred primary input; because their standard deviations are near one, no extra scale transformation is used in the primary result.

**Uncertainties:** The source study's centering/scaling procedure, treatment of PCA observations, and sign convention are not provided.; Eigenvector coefficients describe variable directions in PCA space and are not identical to correlations between variables and PC scores. Sign reversal of a component would reverse all coefficients/scores for that component without changing the PCA.

### 3. Researcher — conclude

Hypothesis: In the reconstructed PCA across 39 century slices, house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords all align positively with PC1. On PC2, house size has the strongest positive loading, daggers a weaker positive loading, and hatchets/swords are near zero but slightly positive. The time-slice scores are distributed on both sides of PC2 rather than concentrated on one side. These are reconstructed results from the supplied Z-values, not asserted to reproduce the undocumented original PCA; PCA component signs are arbitrary.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The reported analysis validated 468 numeric indicator values and performed centered covariance PCA on the 12 supplied Z-value fields. Reported signed eigenvector coefficients support positive PC1 values for all three variables and a PC2 ordering of HouseSize > Dagger > HatchetSword. PC2 scores span negative and positive values. An additional unit-SD scaling check preserved the qualitative loading pattern; original preprocessing and sign conventions remain unknown.

**Open objectives:** None

{
  "id": "call_SPYRIZdS4ZTGjQUedtK6HAlN",
  "responses_item_id": null,
  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute centered and standardized PCA for capital century rows\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p)\\ncols=[c for c in df if c not in ['BCE','group','color']]\\n# Convert source decimal comma strings to numeric dot decimals.\\nX=df[cols].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce'))\\nprint('shape',X.shape,'missing',int(X.isna().sum().sum()),'nonfinite',int((~np.isfinite(X.to_numpy())).sum()))\\nprint('variables',cols)\\nprint('means',X.mean().round(4).to_dict())\\nprint('std sample',X.std(ddof=1).round(4).to_dict())\\n# Primary: supplied Z-valued indicators, mean-centered, no additional scaling.\\nraw=PCA(n_components=12).fit(X)\\n# Sensitivity: standardize columns to unit sample variance, then PCA.\\nZ=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\nstd=PCA(n_components=12).fit(Z)\\nfor label,model,mat in [('raw',raw,X),('standardized',std,pd.DataFrame(Z,columns=cols))]:\\n  # Exact convention: loadings are eigenvector coefficients; PCA sklearn signs canonicalized by svd_flip, but explicitly orient PC1\\n  # so HouseSize coefficient positive; PC2 so HouseSize coefficient positive (if nonzero).\\n  L=model.components_.T.copy()\\n  orient=[]\\n  for k,target in [(0,'Zhausgr'),(1,'Zhausgr')]:\\n    s=1 if L[cols.index(target),k]>=0 else -1\\n    L[:,k]*=s; orient.append(s)\\n  print('\\\\n',label,'orient multipliers',orient,'variance',model.explained_variance_ratio_[:4].round(6).tolist())\\n  for var in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n    print(var,'PC1 coefficient',round(L[cols.index(var),0],6),'PC2 coefficient',round(L[cols.index(var),1],6))\\n  scores=model.transform(mat if label=='raw' else Z)\\n  scores[:,0]*=orient[0]; when standardized, A has already been scaled, then recentered for numerical consistency.\\n    Ac=A-A.mean(axis=0)\\n    cov=Ac.T@Ac/(Ac.shape[0]-1)\\n    vals,vecs=np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\n    ix=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[ix]; V=vecs[:,ix]\\n    # Sign convention for reporting: HouseSize loading positive on each of PC1 and PC2.\\n    signs=[]\\n    for j in [0,1]:\\n        s=1 if V[cols.index('Zhausgr'),j]>=0 else -1\\n        V[:,j]*=s;   "command": "cat > /tmp/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\np='data/capital.csv'\ndf=pd.read_csv(p)\ncols=[c for c in df if c not in ['BCE','group','color']]\n# Convert source decimal comma strings to numeric dot decimals.\nX=df[cols].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce'))\nprint('shape',X.shape,'missing',int(X.isna().sum().sum()),'nonfinite',int((~np.isfinite(X.to_numpy())).sum()))\nprint('variables',cols)\nprint('means',X.mean().round(4).to_dict())\nprint('std sample',X.std(ddof=1).round(4).to_dict())\n# Primary: supplied Z-valued indicators, mean-centered, no additional scaling.\nraw=PCA(n_components=12).fit(X)\n# Sensitivity: standardize columns to unit sample variance, then PCA.\nZ=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\nstd=PCA(n_components=12).fit(Z)\nfor label,model,mat in [('raw',raw,X),('standardized',std,pd.DataFrame(Z,columns=cols))]:\n  # Exact convention: loadings are eigenvector coefficients; PCA sklearn signs canonicalized by svd_flip, but explicitly orient PC1\n  # so HouseSize coefficient positive; PC2 so HouseSize coefficient positive (if nonzero).\n  L=model.components_.T.copy()\n  orient=[]\n  for k,target in [(0,'Zhausgr'),(1,'Zhausgr')]:\n    s=1 if L[cols.index(target),k]>=0 else -1\n    L[:,k]*=s; orient.append(s)\n  print('\\n',label,'orient multipliers',orient,'variance',model.explained_variance_ratio_[:4].round(6).tolist())\n  for var in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\n    print(var,'PC1 coefficient',round(L[cols.index(var),0],6),'PC2 coefficient',round(L[cols.index(var),1],6))\n  scores=model.transform(mat if label=='raw' else Z)\n  scores[:,0]*=orient[0]; scores[:,1]*=orient[1]\n  print('PC2 scores range',round(scores[:,1].min(),5),round(scores[:,1].max(),5),'mean',round(scores[:,1].mean(),7),'sd',round(scores[:,1].std(ddof=1),5))\n  print('PC2 quartiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]).round(5).tolist())\n  print('scores by BCE')\n  for year,sc in zip(df.BCE,scores[:,:2]): print(int(year),*(round(float(v),5) for v in sc))\nPY\npython /tmp/capital_pca.py",

}

{
  "command": "cat > /tmp/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\n\ndf=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv')\ncols=[c for c in df if c not in ['BCE','group','color']]\nX=df[cols].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce')).to_numpy(float)\nprint('shape',X.shape,'missing',int(np.isnan(X).sum()),'nonfinite',int((~np.isfinite(X)).sum()))\nprint('variables',cols)\nprint('means',np.mean(X,axis=0).round(4).tolist())\nprint('sample SD',np.std(X,axis=0,ddof=1).round(4).tolist())\n\ndef pca(A,label):\n    # PCA on centered input; when standardized, A has already been scaled, then recentered for numerical consistency.\n    Ac=A-A.mean(axis=0)\n    cov=Ac.T@Ac/(Ac.shape[0]-1)\n    vals,vecs=np.linalg.eigh(cov)\n    ix=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[ix]; V=vecs[:,ix]\n    # Sign convention for reporting: HouseSize loading positive on each of PC1 and PC2.\n    signs=[]\n    for j in [0,1]:\n        s=1 if V[cols.index('Zhausgr'),j]>=0 else -1\n        V[:,j]*=s; signs.append(s)\n    scores=Ac@V\n    print('\\n'+label,'flip multipliers',signs,'variance ratio first four',(vals[:4]/vals.sum()).round(6).tolist())\n    print('eigenvalues first four',vals[:4].round(6).tolist())\n    print('var | PC1 coeff | PC2 coeff')\n    for var in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\n        print(var,round(V[cols.index(var),0],6),round(V[cols.index(var),1],6))\n    print('PC2 score range, mean, sample SD',round(scores[:,1].min(),5),round(scores[:,1].max(),5),round(scores[:,1].mean(),7),round(scores[:,1].std(ddof=1),5))\n    print('PC2 score quartiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]).round(5).tolist())\n    print('BCE PC1 PC2')\n    for y,s in zip(df.BCE,scores): print(int(y),round(s[0],5),round(s[1],5))\n\npca(X,'PRIMARY: centered supplied Z values, no rescaling')\nXs=(X-X.mean(axis=0))/X.std(axis=0,ddof=1)\npca(Xs,'SENSITIVITY: centered and unit-sample-SD scaled')\nPY\npython /tmp/capital_pca.py",
  "timeout_seconds": 60
}

Cultural Capital consists of Diversity of Pottery form, Diversity of Pottery Decoration. 4. Economic Capital consists of Depot, Sickle, Axes & Celts, Monument Size. 5. Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7. concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.

invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.

include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.

nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not

        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Compute centered and standardized PCA for capital century rows\",\"command\":\"cat > /tmp/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p)\\ncols=[c for c in df if c not in ['BCE','group','color']]\\n# Convert source decimal comma strings to numeric dot decimals.\\nX=df[cols].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce'))\\nprint('shape',X.shape,'missing',int(X.isna().sum().sum()),'nonfinite',int((~np.isfinite(X.to_numpy())).sum()))\\nprint('variables',cols)\\nprint('means',X.mean().round(4).to_dict())\\nprint('std sample',X.std(ddof=1).round(4).to_dict())\\n# Primary: supplied Z-valued indicators, mean-centered, no additional scaling.\\nraw=PCA(n_components=12).fit(X)\\n# Sensitivity: standardize columns to unit sample variance, then PCA.\\nZ=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\nstd=PCA(n_components=12).fit(Z)\\nfor label,model,mat in [('raw',raw,X),('standardized',std,pd.DataFrame(Z,columns=cols))]:\\n  # Exact convention: loadings are eigenvector coefficients; PCA sklearn signs canonicalized by svd_flip, but explicitly orient PC1\\n  # so HouseSize coefficient positive; PC2 so HouseSize coefficient positive (if nonzero).\\n  L=model.components_.T.copy()\\n  orient=[]\\n  for k,target in [(0,'Zhausgr'),(1,'Zhausgr')]:\\n    s=1 if L[cols.index(target),k]>=0 else -1\\n    L[:,k]*=s; orient.append(s)\\n  print('\\\\n',label,'orient multipliers',orient,'variance',model.explained_variance_ratio_[:4].round(6).tolist())\\n  for var in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n    print(var,'PC1 coefficient',round(L[cols.index(var),0],6),'PC2 coefficient',round(L[cols.index(var),1],6))\\n  scores=model.transform(mat if label=='raw' else Z)\\n  scores[:,0]*=orient[0]; scores[:,1]*=orient[1]\\n  print('PC2 scores range',round(scores[:,1].min(),5),round(scores[:,1].max(),5),'mean',round(scores[:,1].mean(),7),'sd',round(scores[:,1].std(ddof=1),5))\\n  print('PC2 quartiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]).round(5).tolist())\\n  print('scores by BCE')\\n  for year,sc in zip(df.BCE,scores[:,:2]): print(int(year),*(round(float(v),5) for v in sc))\\nPY\\npython /tmp/capital_pca.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":60}"

        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute centered and standardized PCA for capital century rows\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p)\\ncols=[c for c in df if c not in ['BCE','group','color']]\\n# Convert source decimal comma strings to numeric dot decimals.\\nX=df[cols].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce'))\\nprint('shape',X.shape,'missing',int(X.isna().sum().sum()),'nonfinite',int((~np.isfinite(X.to_numpy())).sum()))\\nprint('variables',cols)\\nprint('means',X.mean().round(4).to_dict())\\nprint('std sample',X.std(ddof=1).round(4).to_dict())\\n# Primary: supplied Z-valued indicators, mean-centered, no additional scaling.\\nraw=PCA(n_components=12).fit(X)\\n# Sensitivity: standardize columns to unit sample variance, then PCA.\\nZ=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\nstd=PCA(n_components=12).fit(Z)\\nfor label,model,mat in [('raw',raw,X),('standardized',std,pd.DataFrame(Z,columns=cols))]:\\n  # Exact convention: loadings are eigenvector coefficients; PCA sklearn signs canonicalized by svd_flip, but explicitly orient PC1\\n  # so HouseSize coefficient positive; PC2 so HouseSize coefficient positive (if nonzero).\\n  L=model.components_.T.copy()\\n  orient=[]\\n  for k,target in [(0,'Zhausgr'),(1,'Zhausgr')]:\\n    s=1 if L[cols.index(target),k]>=0 else -1\\n    L[:,k]*=s; orient.append(s)\\n  print('\\\\n',label,'orient multipliers',orient,'variance',model.explained_variance_ratio_[:4].round(6).tolist())\\n  for var in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n    print(var,'PC1 coefficient',round(L[cols.index(var),0],6),'PC2 coefficient',round(L[cols.index(var),1],6))\\n  scores=model.transform(mat if label=='raw' else Z)\\n  scores[:,0]*=orient[0]; scores[:,1]*=orient[1]\\n  print('PC2 scores range',round(scores[:,1].min(),5),round(scores[:,1].max(),5),'mean',round(scores[:,1].mean(),7),'sd',round(scores[:,1].std(ddof=1),5))\\n  print('PC2 quartiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]).round(5).tolist())\\n  print('scores by BCE')\\n  for year,sc in zip(df.BCE,scores[:,:2]): print(int(year),*(round(float(v),5) for v in sc))\\nPY\\npython /tmp/capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}"

when standardized, A has already been scaled, then recentered for numerical consistency.\\n    Ac=A-A.mean(axis=0)\\n    cov=Ac.T@Ac/(Ac.shape[0]-1)\\n    vals,vecs=np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\n    ix=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; PCA sklearn signs canonicalized by svd_flip, but explicitly orient PC1\\n  # so HouseSize coefficient positive; PC2 so HouseSize coefficient positive (if nonzero).\\n  L=model.components_.T.copy()\\n  orient=[]\\n  for k,target in [(0,'Zhausgr'),(1,'Zhausgr')]:\\n    s=1 if L[cols.index(target),k]>=0 else -1\\n    L[:,k]*=s; when standardized, A has already been scaled, then recentered for numerical consistency.\\n    Ac=A-A.mean(axis=0)\\n    cov=Ac.T@Ac/(Ac.shape[0]-1)\\n    vals,vecs=np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\n    ix=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[ix]; V=vecs[:,ix]\\n    # Sign convention for reporting: HouseSize loading positive on each of PC1 and PC2.\\n    signs=[]\\n    for j in [0,1]:\\n        s=1 if V[cols.index('Zhausgr'),j]>=0 else -1\\n        V[:,j]*=s; signs.append(s)\\n    scores=Ac@V\\n    print('\\\\n'+label,'flip multipliers',signs,'variance ratio first four',(vals[:4]/vals.sum()).round(6).tolist())\\n    print('eigenvalues first four',vals[:4].round(6).tolist())\\n    print('var | PC1 coeff | PC2 coeff')\\n    for var in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n        print(var,round(V[cols.index(var),0],6),round(V[cols.index(var),1],6))\\n    print('PC2 score range, mean, sample SD',round(scores[:,1].min(),5),round(scores[:,1].max(),5),round(scores[:,1].mean(),7),round(scores[:,1].std(ddof=1),5))\\n    print('PC2 score quartiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]).round(5).tolist())\\n    print('BCE PC1 PC2')\\n    for y,s in zip(df.BCE,scores): print(int(y),round(s[0],5),round(s[1],5))\\n\\npca(X,'PRIMARY: centered supplied Z values, no rescaling')\\nXs=(X-X.mean(axis=0))/X.std(axis=0,ddof=1)\\npca(Xs,'SENSITIVITY: centered and unit-sample-SD scaled')\\nPY\\npython /tmp/capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}"
